1	mod benches;
2	mod harness;
3	mod overhead;

5	
6	use clap::Parser;
7	
8	#[derive(Parser)]
9	#[command(version, about = "IIAC performance measurement", max_term_width = 80)]
10	struct Cli {
11	    /// Benches to run. Pass 'all' for every registered bench, or
12	    /// one or more names. Run with no args to see the available list.
13	    benches: Vec<String>,
14	
15	    /// Target wall-clock seconds per bench (default 5.0). Auto-sizes
16	    /// iterations and INNER. Mutually exclusive with -D.
17	    #[arg(short = 'd', long, conflicts_with = "total_duration")]
18	    duration: Option<f64>,
19	
20	    /// Target total wall-clock seconds across all requested benches;
21	    /// budget is split equally per bench. Mutually exclusive with -d.
22	    #[arg(short = 'D', long)]
23	    total_duration: Option<f64>,
24	
25	    /// Override iterations (skips auto-sizing of total count; INNER still adapts).
26	    #[arg(short, long)]
27	    iterations: Option<u64>,
28	
29	    /// Override INNER (skips auto-sizing of inner-loop count).
30	    /// INNER=1 measures single-call latency (each sample = one step); higher
31	    /// INNER measures back-to-back/burst rate (each sample = N steps averaged).
32	    #[arg(short = 'I', long)]
33	    inner: Option<u64>,
34	
35	    /// Pin bench threads to logical CPUs (comma-separated, ranges OK).
36	    /// The list is a core *pool*: thread `i` of a bench is pinned to
37	    /// `pool[i % pool.len()]`, so shorter pools oversubscribe by wrap.
38	    /// Examples: `--pin 0,1` (2 threads → 2 CPUs), `--pin 0-5` (6-thread
39	    /// pool), `--pin 0,0` (two threads on the same CPU). On 3900X,
40	    /// logical CPUs N and N+12 are SMT siblings of the same physical
41	    /// core — `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max contention), `--pin 0,1`
42	    /// gives independent cores. Omit to leave threads unpinned.
43	    #[arg(long, value_name = "CORES")]
44	    pin: Option<String>,
45	}
46	
47	const DEFAULT_DURATION: f64 = 5.0;
48	
49	fn main() {
50	    let cli = Cli::parse();
51	
52	    if cli.benches.is_empty() {
53	        println!("no benches specified. use -h or --help for more info.");
54	        println!("available: all, {}", benches::names().join(", "));
55	        return;
56	    }
57	
58	    println!(
59	        "iiac-perf {} — IIAC performance measurement\n",
60	        env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")
61	    );
62	
63	    let pin_cores: Vec<usize> = match cli.pin.as_deref() {
64	        None => Vec::new(),
65	        Some(spec) => match pin::parse_cores(spec) {
66	            Ok(v) => v,
67	            Err(e) => {
68	                eprintln!("error: --pin: {e}");
69	                std::process::exit(2);
70	            }
71	        },
72	    };
73	
74	    // Pin the main (measurement) thread before any calibration or bench
75	    // work so TSC reads stay on the same CPU across start/elapsed.
76	    if let Some(&cpu) = pin_cores.first() {
77	        pin::pin_current(Some(cpu));
78	    }
79	
80	    let overhead = overhead::calibrate();
81	    println!("Calibration:");
82	    println!(
83	        "  framing/sample    {:>7} ns  (timer pair, two-point fit)",
84	        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.framing_per_sample_ns, 2)
85	    );
86	    println!(
87	        "  loop/iter         {:>7} ns  (per inner-loop iteration)",
88	        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.loop_per_iter_ns, 2)
89	    );
90	    println!("  pinning           {}", pin::plan_summary(&pin_cores));
91	    println!();
92	
93	    let runners = match benches::resolve(&cli.benches) {
94	        Ok(r) => r,
95	        Err(e) => {
96	            eprintln!("error: {e}");
97	            std::process::exit(2);
98	        }
99	    };
100	
101	    let target_seconds = match (cli.duration, cli.total_duration) {
102	        (Some(d), _) => d,
103	        (None, Some(t)) => t / runners.len() as f64,
104	        (None, None) => DEFAULT_DURATION,
105	    };
106	
107	    let cfg = harness::RunCfg {
108	        overhead: &overhead,
109	        target_seconds,
110	        iterations_override: cli.iterations,
111	        inner_override: cli.inner,
112	        pin_cores: &pin_cores,
113	    };
114	
115	    for run in runners {
116	        run(&cfg);
117	    }
118	}
119	
```

1	pub mod min_now;
2	pub mod mpsc_1t;
3	pub mod mpsc_2t;
4	pub mod std_now;
5	
6	use crate::harness::RunCfg;
7	
8	pub type RunFn = fn(&RunCfg);
9	
10	pub const REGISTRY: &[(&str, RunFn)] = &[
11	    (min_now::NAME, min_now::run),
12	    (std_now::NAME, std_now::run),
13	    (mpsc_1t::NAME, mpsc_1t::run),
14	    (mpsc_2t::NAME, mpsc_2t::run),
15	];
16	
17	pub fn names() -> Vec<&'static str> {
18	    REGISTRY.iter().map(|(n, _)| *n).collect()
19	}
20	
21	pub fn resolve(requested: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<RunFn>, String> {
22	    let want_all = requested.iter().any(|n| n == "all");
23	    let names: Vec<&str> = if want_all {
24	        names()
25	    } else {
26	        requested.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
27	    };
28	
29	    let mut runners = Vec::with_capacity(names.len());
30	    for name in names {
31	        match REGISTRY.iter().find(|(n, _)| *n == name) {
32	            Some((_, run)) => runners.push(*run),
33	            None => {
34	                return Err(format!(
35	                    "unknown bench '{name}'. valid: all, {}",
36	                    self::names().join(", ")
37	                ));
38	            }
39	        }
40	    }
41	    Ok(runners)
42	}
43

1	use std::hint::black_box;
2	
3	use hdrhistogram::Histogram;
4	
5	use crate::overhead::Overhead;
6	
7	const WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;
8	const ESTIMATE_STEPS: u64 = 1_000;
9	const ESTIMATE_SAMPLES: usize = 5;
10	const FRAMING_DOMINATION_RATIO: f64 = 10.0;
11	const MAX_INNER: u64 = 1_000;
12	const MIN_ITERATIONS: u64 = 1_000;
13	const MAX_ITERATIONS: u64 = 1_000_000_000;
14	
15	pub trait Bench {
16	    fn name(&self) -> &str;
17	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64;
18	}
19	
20	#[derive(Debug)]
21	pub struct RunCfg<'a> {
22	    pub overhead: &'a Overhead,
23	    pub target_seconds: f64,
24	    pub iterations_override: Option<u64>,
25	    pub inner_override: Option<u64>,
26	    /// Core pool for thread pinning. Indexed positionally with wrap-around
27	    /// via `core_for(thread_idx)`; empty means no pinning.
28	    pub pin_cores: &'a [usize],
29	}
30	
31	impl RunCfg<'_> {
32	    /// CPU id for the bench's `thread_idx`-th thread, using wrap-around
33	    /// over the pool. Returns `None` when the pool is empty so callers
34	    /// can treat unpinned and pinned runs uniformly.
35	    pub fn core_for(&self, thread_idx: usize) -> Option<usize> {
36	        if self.pin_cores.is_empty() {
37	            None
38	        } else {
39	            Some(self.pin_cores[thread_idx % self.pin_cores.len()])
40	        }
41	    }
42	}
43	
44	pub fn run_adaptive<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, cfg: &RunCfg) -> (Histogram<u64>, u64, u64, f64) {
45	    for _ in 0..WARMUP {
46	        black_box(bench.step());
47	    }
48	
49	    let step_cost_ns = estimate_step_cost(bench);
50	    let framing_ns = cfg.overhead.framing_per_sample_ns.max(1.0);
51	    let inner = cfg
52	        .inner_override
53	        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_inner(step_cost_ns, framing_ns));
54	    let iterations = cfg
55	        .iterations_override
56	        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_iterations(step_cost_ns, inner, cfg.target_seconds));
57	
58	    let (hist, duration_s) = run_bench(bench, iterations, inner);
59	    (hist, iterations, inner, duration_s)
60	}
61	
62	fn estimate_step_cost<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B) -> f64 {
63	    let mut samples: Vec<f64> = (0..ESTIMATE_SAMPLES)
64	        .map(|_| {
65	            let start = minstant::Instant::now();
66	            for _ in 0..ESTIMATE_STEPS {
67	                black_box(bench.step());
68	            }
69	            let total = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
70	            total as f64 / ESTIMATE_STEPS as f64
71	        })
72	        .collect();
73	    samples.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());
74	    samples[ESTIMATE_SAMPLES / 2]
75	}
76	
77	fn pick_inner(step_cost_ns: f64, framing_ns: f64) -> u64 {
78	    let target = (FRAMING_DOMINATION_RATIO * framing_ns / step_cost_ns).ceil() as u64;
79	    target.clamp(1, MAX_INNER)
80	}
81	
82	fn pick_iterations(step_cost_ns: f64, inner: u64, target_seconds: f64) -> u64 {
83	    let target_ns = target_seconds * 1e9;
84	    let per_sample_ns = inner as f64 * step_cost_ns;
85	    let raw = (target_ns / per_sample_ns).ceil() as u64;
86	    raw.clamp(MIN_ITERATIONS, MAX_ITERATIONS)
87	}
88	
89	fn run_bench<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, iterations: u64, inner: u64) -> (Histogram<u64>, f64) {
90	    // 1 ns to 60 s, 3 sig figs
91	    let mut hist = Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 60_000_000_000, 3).unwrap();
92	
93	    let run_start = minstant::Instant::now();
94	    for _ in 0..iterations {
95	        let start = minstant::Instant::now();
96	        for _ in 0..inner {
97	            black_box(bench.step());
98	        }
99	        let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
100	        hist.record(round_elapsed(elapsed_ns, inner)).unwrap();
101	    }
102	    let duration_s = run_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as f64 / 1e9;
103	
104	    (hist, duration_s)
105	}
106	
107	fn round_elapsed(elapsed_ns: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {
108	    (elapsed_ns + inner / 2) / inner
109	}
110	
111	pub fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {
112	    let s = n.to_string();
113	    let mut result = String::new();
114	    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {
115	        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {
116	            result.push(',');
117	        }
118	        result.push(c);
119	    }
120	    result.chars().rev().collect()
121	}
122	
123	pub fn fmt_commas_f64(n: f64, decimals: usize) -> String {
124	    let s = format!("{n:.decimals$}");
125	    let (sign, body) = match s.strip_prefix('-') {
126	        Some(rest) => ("-", rest),
127	        None => ("", s.as_str()),
128	    };
129	    let (int_part, frac_part) = match body.find('.') {
130	        Some(i) => (&body[..i], &body[i..]),
131	        None => (body, ""),
132	    };
133	    let int_num: u64 = int_part.parse().unwrap_or(0);
134	    format!("{sign}{}{frac_part}", fmt_commas(int_num))
135	}
136	
137	pub fn print_histogram(
138	    name: &str,
139	    iterations: u64,
140	    inner: u64,
141	    duration_s: f64,
142	    hist: &Histogram<u64>,
143	    overhead: &Overhead,
144	) {
145	    // Header line: bench name + logfmt-style metadata. `adj` is the
146	    // apparatus overhead subtracted from each sample downstream.
147	    let adj = overhead.per_call_ns(inner);
148	    let total = iterations * inner;
149	    println!(
150	        "{name} [duration={:.1}s outer={} inner={} calls={} adj/call={}ns]:",
151	        duration_s,
152	        fmt_commas(iterations),
153	        inner,
154	        fmt_commas(total),
155	        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 2),
156	    );
157	
158	    // Band boundaries defined by percentiles. Each consecutive pair
159	    // forms one band; we iterate the histogram to compute per-band
160	    // stats (first, last, count, mean).
161	    let boundary_pcts: &[f64] = &[
162	        0.0, 0.01, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.99, 1.0,
163	    ];
164	    let boundary_names: &[&str] = &[
165	        "min", "p1", "p10", "p20", "p30", "p40", "p50", "p60", "p70", "p80", "p90", "p99", "max",
166	    ];
167	
168	    let n_bands = boundary_pcts.len() - 1;
169	    let sample_count = hist.len();
170	
171	    // Accumulate per-band stats by walking recorded histogram buckets.
172	    // Each bucket is assigned to the band containing its midpoint rank.
173	    let mut band_first = vec![u64::MAX; n_bands];
174	    let mut band_last = vec![0u64; n_bands];
175	    let mut band_count = vec![0u64; n_bands];
176	    let mut band_sum = vec![0u128; n_bands];
177	
178	    let mut cumulative = 0u64;
179	    for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {
180	        let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
181	        let count = iv.count_at_value();
182	        let mid_rank = (cumulative as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
183	        let idx = boundary_pcts[1..]
184	            .iter()
185	            .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
186	            .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
187	        band_first[idx] = band_first[idx].min(value);
188	        band_last[idx] = band_last[idx].max(value);
189	        band_count[idx] += count;
190	        band_sum[idx] += value as u128 * count as u128;
191	        cumulative += count;
192	    }
193	
194	    // Build rendered rows: (label, first, last, count, mean, adj_mean).
195	    struct BandRow {
196	        label: String,
197	        first: String,
198	        last: String,
199	        count: String,
200	        mean: String,
201	        adj_mean: String,
202	    }
203	
204	    let mut rows: Vec<BandRow> = Vec::new();
205	    for i in 0..n_bands {
206	        if band_count[i] == 0 {
207	            continue;
208	        }
209	        let mean_val = band_sum[i] as f64 / band_count[i] as f64;
210	        let adj_mean = (mean_val - adj).max(0.0);
211	        rows.push(BandRow {
212	            label: format!("{}-{}", boundary_names[i], boundary_names[i + 1]),
213	            first: fmt_commas(band_first[i]),
214	            last: fmt_commas(band_last[i]),
215	            count: fmt_commas(band_count[i]),
216	            mean: fmt_commas_f64(mean_val, 0),
217	            adj_mean: fmt_commas_f64(adj_mean, 0),
218	        });
219	    }
220	
221	    // Column widths from rendered strings.
222	    let label_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.label.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
223	    let first_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.first.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
224	    let last_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.last.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
225	    let count_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.count.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
226	    let mean_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
227	    let adj_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.adj_mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
228	
229	    const INDENT: &str = "  ";
230	    const GAP: &str = "    ";
231	
232	    // Header row.
233	    let first_col = INDENT.len() + label_w + 1 + first_w;
234	    let last_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + last_w;
235	    let count_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + count_w;
236	    let mean_gap = GAP.len() + mean_w;
237	    let adj_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + adj_w;
238	    println!(
239	        "{:>first_col$}{:>last_gap$}{:>count_gap$}{:>mean_gap$}{:>adj_gap$}",
240	        "first", "last", "count", "mean", "adjusted",
241	    );
242	
243	    for r in &rows {
244	        println!(
245	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>first_w$} ns{GAP}{:>last_w$} ns{GAP}{:>count_w$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns{GAP}{:>adj_w$} ns",
246	            r.label, r.first, r.last, r.count, r.mean, r.adj_mean,
247	        );
248	    }
249	
250	    // Whole-histogram summary. Aligned to mean/adjusted columns.
251	    let hist_mean = hist.mean();
252	    let hist_adj = (hist_mean - adj).max(0.0);
253	    let skip = first_w + " ns".len() + GAP.len() + last_w + " ns".len() + GAP.len() + count_w;
254	    println!(
255	        "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns{GAP}{:>adj_w$} ns",
256	        "mean",
257	        "",
258	        fmt_commas_f64(hist_mean, 0),
259	        fmt_commas_f64(hist_adj, 0),
260	    );
261	    println!(
262	        "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns",
263	        "stdev",
264	        "",
265	        fmt_commas_f64(hist.stdev(), 0),
266	    );
267	    println!();
268	}
269	
```

1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	
9	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
10	database to better manage the information, TBD.
11	
12	Examples chore file:
13	```
14	# Chores-01.md
15	
16	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
17	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
18	how and why of a particular chore. The section header is short and sweet
19	and the title is appended with the version number of the app when the chore
20	is completed.
21	
22	## Create an app that does something interesting (0.1.0)
23	
24	The app counts from 1 to 100, not to interesting.
25	```
26	
27	## Commit Workflow
28	
29	This project uses a per-step commit / push / finalize flow against
30	two paired repos (app + bot session). Full rules live in
31	[CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) — that file is
32	the source of truth (the bot loads it automatically; humans can
33	read it from there too).
34	
35	## jj tips
36	
37	For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
38	
39	```
40	## Chores format
41	
42	Filename: "chores-XX.md"
43	example: chores-01.md
44	
45	Format of section labels: "## <short description> (X.Y.Z)"
46	example: "## Topic format description (0.1.0)"
47	
48	Example chore file:
49	```
50	# Chores-01.md
51	 
52	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
53	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
54	how and why of a particular chore.
55	
56	## Do something (1.3.1)
57	
58	Describe something
59	```
60	
61	## Versioning during development
62	
63	This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
64	git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
65	we go.
66	
67	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
68	
69	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
70	  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
71	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
72	  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
73	
74	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
75	
76	For multi-step:
77	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
78	2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
79	3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
80	
81	The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
82	prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
83	commits were exploratory vs final.
84	
85	## Todo format
86	
87	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
88	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
89	references.
90	
91	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
92	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
93	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
94	
95	Examples:
96	
97	# Todo
98	
99	- Add new feature X [details](chores-01.md#feature-x)
100	- Fix bug Y [1]
101	
102	# Done
103	
104	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
105	
106	[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
107	
```

> AGENT

Got it. Here's the lay of the land:

**iiac-perf** (v0.3.5) is a Rust micro-benchmarking tool for measuring inter-isolate/inter-actor communication (IIAC) performance. It has:

- **4 benchmarks** registered in a trait-based harness:
  - `min_now` / `std_now` — timer call overhead (minstant vs std `Instant::now`)
  - `mpsc_1t` — single-thread `std::sync::mpsc` channel round-trip
  - `mpsc_2t` — two-thread mpsc round-trip

- **Adaptive sizing** — auto-picks iteration count and inner-loop depth to hit a target wall-clock duration while keeping timer framing overhead negligible.

- **HdrHistogram** output with band-based percentile display, overhead calibration, and adjusted-mean columns.

- **CLI** (clap-derive) with `-d`/`-D` duration controls, `-I` inner override, `--pin` CPU affinity, and bench name selection.

**Current uncommitted changes** touch `Cargo.lock`, `Cargo.toml`, `src/benches/mpsc_2t.rs`, `src/harness.rs`, `src/overhead.rs`, and `src/pin.rs`.

**Upcoming work** on the todo list includes crossbeam, tokio-mpsc, and flume channel benchmarks, function-call baselines, and extracting shared helpers once a second channel impl lands.

The project uses **jj (Jujutsu)** for version control with a paired app + bot-session repo workflow, conventional commits with version suffixes, and a structured commit-push-finalize flow.

Ready to work — what's next?

1	use std::collections::BTreeSet;
2	
3	/// Parse a `--pin` value (comma-separated list with optional ranges) into
4	/// an ordered vector of logical CPU IDs. Accepts forms like `"0,1"`,
5	/// `"0-11"`, `"0,3-5,7"`. Duplicates are preserved in the given order so
6	/// oversubscription (`"0,0,0"`) works.
7	pub fn parse_cores(spec: &str) -> Result<Vec<usize>, String> {
8	    let mut out = Vec::new();
9	    for part in spec.split(',').map(str::trim).filter(|s| !s.is_empty()) {
10	        match part.split_once('-') {
11	            None => out.push(
12	                part.parse::<usize>()
13	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid core id {part:?}: {e}"))?,
14	            ),
15	            Some((lo, hi)) => {
16	                let lo = lo
17	                    .trim()
18	                    .parse::<usize>()
19	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid range start {lo:?}: {e}"))?;
20	                let hi = hi
21	                    .trim()
22	                    .parse::<usize>()
23	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid range end {hi:?}: {e}"))?;
24	                if hi < lo {
25	                    return Err(format!("range {lo}-{hi} is empty"));
26	                }
27	                out.extend(lo..=hi);
28	            }
29	        }
30	    }
31	    Ok(out)
32	}
33	
34	/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None` or if the CPU
35	/// id isn't present on this machine.
36	pub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {
37	    println!("pin_current: logical_cpu={logical_cpu:?}");
38	    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };
39	    let Some(ids) = core_affinity::get_core_ids() else {
40	        return;
41	    };
42	    println!("pin_current: ids={ids:?}");
43	    if let Some(id) = ids.into_iter().find(|c| c.id == target) {
44	        println!("pin_current: set_for_current({id:?}");
45	        core_affinity::set_for_current(id);
46	    }
47	}
48	
49	/// Report the pinning plan (what the user asked for) as a human-readable
50	/// summary for the startup banner.
51	pub fn plan_summary(cores: &[usize]) -> String {
52	    if cores.is_empty() {
53	        return "none (unpinned)".to_string();
54	    }
55	    let unique: BTreeSet<usize> = cores.iter().copied().collect();
56	    format!(
57	        "{cores:?} ({} slot{}, {} unique CPU{})",
58	        cores.len(),
59	        if cores.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
60	        unique.len(),
61	        if unique.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
62	    )
63	}
64

> AGENT

The bug is exactly as you described. `core_affinity::get_core_ids()` returns the current thread's affinity mask, not all system cores. After the main thread is pinned to CPU 6, subsequent calls only see `[CoreId { id: 6 }]`, so pinning the worker to CPU 10 silently fails.

The fix: construct a `CoreId` directly instead of looking it up via `get_core_ids()`. The `CoreId` struct is just a wrapper around an `id: usize` field — no need to query the OS for the list.

> TOOL

id: toolu_01NGd126kbTS7KMozVFmy12q
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/pin.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None` or if the CPU\n/// id isn't present on this machine.\npub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {\n    println!(\"pin_current: logical_cpu={logical_cpu:?}\");\n    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };\n    let Some(ids) = core_affinity::get_core_ids() else {\n        return;\n    };\n    println!(\"pin_current: ids={ids:?}\");\n    if let Some(id) = ids.into_iter().find(|c| c.id == target) {\n        println!(\"pin_current: set_for_current({id:?}\");\n        core_affinity::set_for_current(id);\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None`.\n///\n/// Constructs a `CoreId` directly rather than querying\n/// `get_core_ids()`, which only returns cores in the caller's\n/// current affinity mask — after the first pin that mask is\n/// narrowed and subsequent lookups for other cores would fail.\npub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {\n    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };\n    core_affinity::set_for_current(core_affinity::CoreId { id: target });\n}"
}

> AGENT

All clean. The fix: instead of calling `core_affinity::get_core_ids()` (which reflects the calling thread's current affinity mask), we now construct a `CoreId { id: target }` directly and pass it to `set_for_current`. After the main thread pins itself to CPU 6, `get_core_ids()` was returning only `[CoreId { id: 6 }]`, so the worker thread's attempt to pin to CPU 10 silently failed.

> AGENT

Good — the `after pin_current: cid=10` confirms the worker thread is now actually landing on CPU 10. Previously that line still showed `cid=5`.

The 60%-ish utilization on cores 6 and 11 (which are the System Monitor's 1-indexed view of logical CPUs 5 and 10) makes sense — mpsc is a ping-pong: each thread sends then blocks waiting for a reply, so neither thread can saturate its core. The `user=4.6s` vs `sys=15.6s` split over 19.6s real also shows most time is spent in kernel synchronization (futex wake/wait under the channel), not userspace compute.

Ready when you are for versioning / commit.

2	
3	Discussions and notes on various chores in github compatible markdown.
4	There is also a [todo.md](todo.md) file and it tracks tasks and in
5	general there should be a chore section for each task with the why
6	and how this task will be completed.
7	
8	See [Chores format](README.md#chores-format)

10	## A dummy chore (TBD)
11	
12	A dummy chore description.
13	
14	## Measure timer overhead (0.1.0)
15	
16	Added initial Rust app that measures the overhead of `minstant::Instant::now()`
17	vs `std::time::Instant::now()`. Uses `minstant` as the reference timer for both
18	measurements and records 1M iterations into `hdrhistogram` histograms. Reports
19	min, p50, p90, p99, p99.9, p99.99, max, mean, and stdev in nanoseconds.
20	
21	This bootstraps the benchmarking project by measuring the measuring stick first,
22	validating the measurement approach before applying it to actual IIAC techniques.
23	
24	## Refactor to Bench trait + add channel bench (0.2.0)
25	
26	Restructure `iiac-perf` so new IIAC benchmarks can be added as self-contained
27	modules instead of hand-wiring each into `main`. Also add overhead calibration
28	and the first real IIAC bench: `std::sync::mpsc` round-trip on one thread.
29	
30	### Why
31	
32	The README's trajectory (async, serde, io_uring, LAN, WAN, …) implies many
33	benchmarks. A `match` in `main` would grow unwieldy. Calibrating timer and
34	loop overhead lets each bench report a per-call cost closer to the technique
35	itself rather than the measurement apparatus.
36	
37	### How
38	
39	Multi-step, target version `0.2.0`. Approach approved by user.
40	
41	Planned module layout:
42	
43	```
44	src/
45	  main.rs              # CLI: `iiac-perf [timer|channel|all] -i N`
46	  harness.rs           # Bench trait + runner + histogram printer
47	  overhead.rs          # timer + loop overhead calibration
48	  benches/
49	    mod.rs
50	    timer.rs           # minstant vs std::time::Instant
51	    channel.rs         # std::sync::mpsc round trip, single thread
52	```
53	
54	Trait sketch:
55	
56	```rust
57	pub trait Bench {
58	    fn name(&self) -> &str;
59	    fn step(&mut self);  // one unit of work; harness times N per sample
60	}
61	```
62	
63	Overhead subtraction:
64	
65	- `timer_overhead_ns` — min of back-to-back `Instant::now() / elapsed()` pairs
66	- `loop_overhead_ns`  — per-iter cost of empty `for _ in 0..N { black_box(()); }`
67	- `per_call = (elapsed - timer_overhead)/N - loop_overhead`
68	
69	Print **raw and adjusted side-by-side**. Subtracting a min from a full
70	distribution is asymmetric — near the floor, adjusted percentiles can go
71	near zero. Showing both keeps the raw data honest.
72	
73	### Dev steps
74	
75	1. `0.2.0-dev1` ✅ chore marker: bump version, write this plan, update todo.
76	   Also clarified CLAUDE.md: commit-push-finalize runs per step.
77	2. `0.2.0-dev2` ✅ refactor: `Bench` trait + `harness` module, `benches/timer.rs`.
78	   `run_bench` is generic (`<B: Bench>`) so monomorphization keeps the hot
79	   loop free of vtable dispatch — matters for a perf tool. No behavior
80	   change vs 0.1.0 output.
81	3. `0.2.0-dev3` ✅ apparatus calibration: run `EmptyBench` (returning `u32`,
82	   `black_box`-wrapped at the call site) through a dedicated calibration
83	   loop with `CALIBRATION_INNER = 100` and take per-sample min. Single
84	   `apparatus/call` number subtracted from each raw value to produce the
85	   adjusted column. Notes from the experiment:
86	   - First attempt with INNER=10 + `step()->()` produced 0 ns: TSC reads
87	     pipeline when there's nothing serial between them.
88	   - `black_box(())` doesn't introduce a serial dependency (ZST has
89	     nothing to hold). Required `step()->u32` so `black_box(value)`
90	     anchors the work.
91	   - With matching N=10, even with non-trivial step return, total
92	     framed work (~6 ns) still rounds to 0 under TSC granularity.
93	   - Decoupling calibration N (=100) from harness N (=10) gives a
94	     measurable per-call number (~0.6 ns) without changing the bench
95	     loop. Slight under-subtract of framing-per-call (~1 ns) accepted
96	     as honest noise floor; revisit if/when a bench needs precision.
97	4. `0.2.0-dev4` ✅ first IIAC bench: `benches/channel.rs` —
98	   `StdMpscRoundTrip` keeps both `Sender` and `Receiver` on one thread,
99	   sends a `u64` then `recv()`s it. CLI gained subcommands: `timer`,
100	   `channel`, `all` (default when no subcommand). On 3900x: round-trip
101	   min 20 ns, p50 26 ns, p99 31 ns at 100M calls — close to
102	   `std::time::Instant::now()` cost since single-thread mpsc has no
103	   contention/blocking. (Note: `std::sync::mpsc` is implemented on top
104	   of `crossbeam-channel` since Rust ~1.67, so this is effectively
105	   measuring crossbeam through the std API.)
106	   Also widened `Bench::step` return type from `u32` to `u64` — drops
107	   the `as u32` truncation in channel.rs since the message is `u64`,
108	   and matches future benches that naturally produce `u64`
109	   (timestamps, sizes, pointers). Cost on 32-bit CPUs is one extra
110	   register move per call, negligible.
111	5. `0.2.0` ✅ finalize: drop `-devN`, move todo entry to Done.
112	
113	## Multi-thread mpsc + per-bench files + named CLI (0.3.0)
114	
115	Add the next obvious IIAC bench (cross-thread mpsc round-trip) and
116	restructure so each bench impl lives in its own file. Replace the
117	fixed `timer`/`channel`/`all` subcommands with a positional list of
118	bench names dispatched via a registry — composes naturally as more
119	benches arrive.
120	
121	### Why
122	
123	After 0.2.0, `benches/timer.rs` already held two unrelated `Bench`
124	impls (minstant vs std::time). Future channel variants (crossbeam,
125	tokio, flume, …) would compound that. One file per impl scales
126	without forcing umbrella refactors. The registry-driven CLI
127	(`iiac-perf min-now mpsc-2t`) lets users pick exactly what they
128	want without hand-coded subcommands per bench.
129	
130	### How
131	
132	File layout:
133	```
134	src/benches/
135	  mod.rs        # REGISTRY: &[(name, RunFn)]
136	  min_now.rs    # NAME = "min-now"
137	  std_now.rs    # NAME = "std-now"
138	  mpsc_1t.rs    # NAME = "mpsc-1t"  (was channel.rs / StdMpscRoundTrip)
139	  mpsc_2t.rs    # NAME = "mpsc-2t"  (added in dev2)
140	```
141	
142	Each bench module exposes `pub const NAME: &str` and
143	`pub fn run(iterations: u64, overhead: &Overhead)`.
144	
145	CLI: positional `Vec<String>`. Empty → print one-line help (suggesting
146	`-h`/`--help`) plus the available bench list, exit 0 — avoids
147	accidentally launching all benches with a bare `iiac-perf`. `all` →
148	run every bench in registry order. Unknown name → friendly error
149	listing valid options.
150	
151	Breaking CLI change vs 0.2.0 (no more `timer`/`channel` subcommands)
152	— acceptable on a 0.x release.
153	
154	### Dev steps
155	
156	1. `0.3.0-dev1` — split timer into `min_now.rs` + `std_now.rs`,
157	   rename `channel.rs` → `mpsc_1t.rs`, add registry + named-list CLI.
158	   No new bench, no behavior change beyond the CLI shape.
159	2. `0.3.0-dev2` ✅ added `mpsc_2t.rs` — `StdMpsc2Thread` spawns a
160	   worker thread, uses two `mpsc::channel`s (request + response).
161	   `step()` sends a `u64` then `recv()`s the echo. Clean shutdown
162	   in `Drop`: `mem::replace` swaps `req_tx` for a dummy sender so
163	   the real one drops, the worker's `recv()` returns Err and the
164	   thread exits and joins. Using replace (not `Option<Sender>`)
165	   keeps `step()` branch-free.
166	
167	   Results on 3900x at 10M iterations: **min 270 ns / p50 7,159 ns /
168	   max 672 µs — ~275× slower than `mpsc-1t` (26 ns p50)**. Bimodal:
169	   best case ~300 ns when both threads are hot and don't park; typical
170	   ~7 µs once Linux futex parking/wakeup kicks in. Demonstrates the
171	   threading cost for tiny messages — every round trip pays two
172	   wakeups.
173	
174	   Also appended future bench candidates to todo (crossbeam-channel,
175	   tokio::mpsc, flume, function-call baselines).
176	3. `0.3.0-dev3` ✅ adaptive INNER + iteration sizing + apparatus split.
177	   Default 10M iterations was 70 s for `mpsc-2t`; now each bench
178	   targets ~1 s by default.
179	   - Warmup estimates per-step cost (median of 5 × 1000 steps).
180	   - **INNER** = `max(1, ceil(k × framing / step_cost))`, k = 10 —
181	     bench dominates framing 10×. On 3900x: min-now INNER=13,
182	     std-now/mpsc-1t INNER=5, mpsc-2t INNER=1.
183	   - **iterations** = `target_seconds / (INNER × step_cost)`, default
184	     target 1.0 s. CLI: `-d/--duration SECONDS` (default 1.0),
185	     `-i/--iterations N` overrides total count (INNER still adapts).
186	   - **Apparatus split**: `Overhead { framing_per_sample_ns,
187	     loop_per_iter_ns }`. Two-point fit on EmptyBench at N=100 and
188	     N=1000 — slope = loop_per_iter, intercept = framing. Cancels
189	     TSC pipelining of the framing pair (which made single-point
190	     measurement read 0). Subtracted as
191	     `framing/INNER + loop_per_iter`, honest at any INNER.
192	   - 3900x calibration: framing 11.11 ns, loop_iter 0.49 ns.
193	     mpsc-2t with INNER=1 now shows the bimodal distribution
194	     directly: min 280 ns (hot), p1 6 µs (typical with futex park),
195	     max 1.4 ms (jitter spike).
196	
197	   ### INNER=1 vs INNER=N — what's actually being measured
198	
199	   Comparing dev2 (`mpsc-2t -i 100000` → INNER=10, 1M round-trips,
200	   mean 5,600 ns) against dev3 (`mpsc-2t` → INNER=1, ~113k samples,
201	   mean 8,500 ns) revealed a meaningful semantic shift:
202	
203	   - **INNER=N** measures **back-to-back rate**. Inside one sample
204	     the main thread never pauses, so the worker often hasn't parked
205	     yet from the previous round-trip — subsequent round-trips skip
206	     the futex wake. The reported mean is amortized over hot bursts.
207	     `min 274 ns` translates to "10 round-trips averaged 274 ns each"
208	     — real burst behavior, not single-RT cost.
209	   - **INNER=1** measures **isolated single-call latency**. Every
210	     sample has a `now()/record` pause between round-trips, giving
211	     the worker time to park. Most round-trips pay the wake. The
212	     bimodal distribution becomes visible: rare hot RTs (~190 ns)
213	     vs typical parked RTs (~8 µs).
214	
215	   INNER=1 is the truer per-call latency a real caller pays.
216	   INNER=N is useful when you specifically want pipelined throughput.
217	   `-I/--inner N` lets the user pick. Future work could add a
218	   dedicated `mpsc-2t-burst` bench fixed at high INNER if the burst
219	   number deserves its own slot.
220	4. `0.3.0` ✅ finalize: drop `-devN`, move todo entry to Done.
221	
222	## Tune duration default + add total-duration flag (0.3.1)
223	
224	Empirical follow-up to 0.3.0. User found `-d 60` gives ~0.3 % run-to-run
225	mean swing on mpsc-2t vs ~4 % at `-d 1`, but no data points in between.
226	
227	### Why
228	
229	A reasonable default `-d` should give "good enough" stability without
230	forcing the user to wait long. Also, the current `-d` is per-bench;
231	some workflows want a fixed total wall-clock budget split across the
232	requested benches.
233	
234	### Dev steps
235	
236	1. `0.3.1-dev1` ✅ measured mpsc-2t at `-d` ∈ {1, 3, 5, 10, 30} × 3
237	   runs on 3900x. Picked **default = 5.0** at the knee.
238	
239	   | -d | mean range (ns) | mean swing | p50 range | p99 range |
240	   |----|----------------|-----------|-----------|-----------|
241	   | 1  | 7,377–8,890    | ~18 %     | 7,075–8,847 | 11,047–12,343 |
242	   | 3  | 7,399–8,577    | ~15 %     | 7,263–8,847 | 10,775–11,695 |
243	   | 5  | 6,960–7,611    | **~9 %**  | 6,763–7,363 | 10,319–11,191 |
244	   | 10 | 7,132–8,072    | ~12 %     | 6,843–8,199 | 10,703–11,583 |
245	   | 30 | 7,467–7,711    | ~3 %      | 7,295–7,383 | 10,895–11,127 |
246	
247	   d=5 is the knee: p50 range collapses 1,772→600 ns, mean swing
248	   nearly halves over d=1. d=10 didn't pay back its 2× cost in
249	   this sample (3 runs is small for inference; d=10 would likely
250	   stabilise with more). d=30 is gold-standard but 2 minutes for
251	   `iiac-perf all` is too long for a default. d=5 → ~25 s for `all`,
252	   tolerable.
253	
254	   Default bumped from 1.0 to 5.0 in `main.rs`. Users wanting
255	   publication-grade stability use `-d 30` explicitly; README notes
256	   this.
257	2. `0.3.1-dev2` ✅ added `-D/--total-duration SECONDS`. Splits the
258	   budget equally across requested benches; mutually exclusive with
259	   `-d` via clap `conflicts_with` (clap exits with a friendly error
260	   if both are passed). `-d` is now `Option<f64>` so `conflicts_with`
261	   works correctly without faking explicit set; default falls back to
262	   `DEFAULT_DURATION = 5.0` only when neither flag is given.
263	   `/README.md` Usage updated with the new flag and an `-D 30`
264	   example.
265	
266	   Also fixed `iiac-perf -h` not wrapping long descriptions: clap
267	   needs the `wrap_help` cargo feature (not in default features) to
268	   actually wrap, plus `max_term_width = 80` in `#[command(...)]`
269	   to cap on wide terminals.
270	3. `0.3.1` ✅ finalize: drop `-devN`, move todo entry to Done.
271	
272	## Add duration to bench header + logfmt-style metadata (0.3.2)
273	
274	User asked for the bench header to show the wall-clock duration of
275	the measurement so it's easy to see how long a run actually took.
276	While iterating on format we converged on logfmt-style bracketed
277	`key=value` pairs — self-describing, easy to eyeball, trivial to
278	parse.
279	
280	### Change
281	
282	- `run_bench` now times the whole sampling loop (not warmup/estimate)
283	  and returns `(Histogram, duration_s)`.
284	- `run_adaptive` threads `duration_s` through to callers; all four
285	  bench `run()` functions pass it to `print_histogram`.
286	- `print_histogram` header switches from
287	  `{name} ({iters} iters × {inner} inner = {calls} calls; subtract {adj} ns/call)`
288	  to
289	  `{name} [duration={:.1}s outer={iters} inner={inner} calls={calls} adj/call={adj}ns]`.
290	
291	### Why logfmt
292	
293	- Brackets visually separate metadata from the name.
294	- Space-separated `key=value` parses with any logfmt lib.
295	- `outer`/`inner` pair more clearly than the old `iters`/`inner`.
296	- `adj/call` is more descriptive than `subtract`.
297	
298	Single-step bump 0.3.1 → 0.3.2 (mechanical change).
299	
300	## Auto-size histogram columns (0.3.3)
301	
302	After 0.3.2 the user hand-tuned fixed column widths in `print_histogram`
303	because values with commas (e.g. `1,701,887 ns`) no longer fit the
304	previous `{:>10}` fields. Fixed widths are fragile — they overflow on
305	big `max` values and leave excess whitespace on small runs.
306	
307	### Change
308	
309	`print_histogram` now:
310	
311	1. Builds a unified `Vec<Row>` where `Row { label, raw: f64, adj:
312	   Option<f64> }` covers every displayed stat. Constructors are
313	   `Row::with_adj` (percentiles / min / max / mean — with overhead
314	   subtraction) and `Row::raw_only` (stdev, where overhead
315	   subtraction on a spread is meaningless).
316	2. All values render with 0 decimals (whole ns). Rationale: timer
317	   resolution is ~1 ns and cross-run variance is much larger than
318	   sub-ns, so extra decimals would overclaim precision. Demonstrated
319	   at `-d 60`: run-to-run `max` varied 2.8×, `mean` ~5 %, duration
320	   itself 31 %. 100 ps precision would be fiction.
321	3. Takes `max(len)` per column from the rendered strings to derive
322	   `raw_w` / `adj_w`.
323	4. Emits the header row right-aligned to the numeric column
324	   right-edges (just before the " ns" suffix).
325	5. Emits each data row using the computed widths; `raw_only` rows
326	   fall through a `match` arm that skips the adjusted column.
327	
328	`print_row` helper removed — rendering is inline with the widths
329	in scope. Section comments added to each phase so the intent of
330	header / rows / widths / output is obvious at a glance.
331	
332	### Why
333	
334	- No more manual width tuning when values get bigger.
335	- Each run's table is internally consistent regardless of scale
336	  (sub-ns to multi-ms).
337	- Adds ~15 lines of code but deletes the brittle hard-coded widths.
338	
339	Single-step bump 0.3.2 → 0.3.3 (mechanical refactor, same visible
340	layout, widths now data-driven).
341	
342	## Add `--pin` CPU affinity flag (0.3.4)
343	
344	Observed that unpinned `mpsc-2t` produces an 80 ns min alongside a
345	3.7 ms max — honest but mixes real measurement with scheduler
346	noise. Thread migration also invalidates TSC reads across CPUs
347	(minstant `start`/`elapsed` may span cores if the kernel moves us
348	mid-measurement), so pinning helps accuracy *and* reproducibility.
349	
350	### Change
351	
352	- New dep: `core_affinity = "0.8"`.
353	- New module: `src/pin.rs` with `parse_cores`, `pin_current`, and
354	  `plan_summary`. Parser accepts commas + ranges + duplicates (e.g.
355	  `0,1`, `0-5`, `0,3-5,7`, `0,0`).
356	- `--pin CORES` CLI flag (comma-separated list / ranges). Treated
357	  as a **core pool**: thread `i` pins to `pool[i % pool.len()]`.
358	  Wrap-around handles oversubscription naturally.
359	- `RunCfg::core_for(thread_idx) -> Option<usize>` helper returns
360	  the pinning target (or `None` when the pool is empty so callers
361	  treat unpinned and pinned uniformly).
362	- `main()` pins the measurement thread to `pool[0]` before
363	  calibration so framing overhead is measured on the same CPU used
364	  for benches.
365	- `mpsc_2t::StdMpsc2Thread::new` takes `worker_cpu: Option<usize>`
366	  and pins the worker on spawn.
367	- Single-thread benches (`min_now`, `std_now`, `mpsc_1t`) inherit
368	  pinning automatically since they run on the (already-pinned)
369	  main thread.
370	- Banner adds `pinning  [0, 1] (2 slots, 2 unique CPUs)` line.
371	
372	### Why logical-CPU terminology
373	
374	`core_affinity`, `taskset`, and the Linux scheduler all operate on
375	*logical* CPU IDs — i.e. SMT threads. The docs previously conflated
376	"core" (physical silicon) with "logical CPU" (OS-visible execution
377	context). On 3900X: 12 physical cores × 2 SMT = 24 logical CPUs.
378	Logical `N` and `N+12` are SMT siblings of the same physical core
379	(confirmed via `/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list`
380	→ `0,12`). README explains the distinction with `--pin 0,1`
381	(independent cores, same CCX) vs `--pin 0,12` (SMT siblings).
382	
383	### Measured effect (3900X, idle desktop, `-d 10`)
384	
385	| pinning | mean | stdev | p99.99 | max |
386	|---------|-----:|------:|-------:|----:|
387	| none | 7,044 ns | 6,545 ns | 74 µs | 3.7 ms |
388	| `0,1` (independent) | 5,636 ns | 1,321 ns | 17 µs | 311 µs |
389	| `0,12` (SMT siblings) | 5,744 ns | 1,476 ns | 16 µs | 66 µs |
390	
391	Stdev dropped ~5×, p99.99 ~4×, mean ~20 %. Max still has kernel
392	preemption events (311 µs / 66 µs with pinning vs 3.7 ms without —
393	even preemption is rarer on pinned cores). Interestingly,
394	`0,12` (SMT siblings) and `0,1` (independent cores) are nearly
395	indistinguishable for this latency-bound round-trip, suggesting
396	SMT helps hide channel-wait latency rather than hurting via
397	resource contention.
398	
399	Single-step bump 0.3.3 → 0.3.4.
400	
401	## Band-based histogram display (0.3.5)
402	
403	Replaced per-percentile rows with per-band rows. Each band is
404	defined by two consecutive percentile boundaries (e.g. min→p1,
405	p1→p10, …, p99→max) and displays first, last, count, mean, and
406	adjusted mean — computed by iterating the histogram's recorded
407	buckets and accumulating per-band stats.
408	
409	### Why
410	
411	Percentile rows associate a single boundary value with a label
412	(e.g. "p99 = 10,095 ns"). When we tried to add per-band sample
413	counts, neither direction of association (prev→current or
414	current→next) produced symmetric tail counts: p1 and p99 always
415	ended up with different band widths depending on which direction
416	was chosen. This caused persistent confusion about what "p99's
417	count" meant.
418	
419	Making the *band* the row (not the *point*) resolved the
420	asymmetry: min-p1 and p99-max are both 1% of total by
421	construction. The range labels are wider but unambiguous — a
422	band IS two boundaries and pretending otherwise was the source
423	of all the confusion.
424	
425	### Change
426	
427	- `print_histogram` now iterates `hist.iter_recorded()` to
428	  accumulate per-band first/last/count/sum. Each histogram
429	  bucket is assigned to the band containing its midpoint rank.
430	- Band boundaries: 0%, 1%, 10%, 20%, …, 90%, 99%, 100%.
431	  Labels: min-p1, p1-p10, p10-p20, …, p90-p99, p99-max.
432	- Display columns: first, last, count, mean, adjusted.
433	  Auto-sized column widths (same pattern as 0.3.3).
434	- Whole-histogram mean and stdev shown below the band table
435	  without a count column.
436	- Removed the old `Row` struct and its constructors — rendering
437	  is now done via a local `BandRow` struct inside
438	  `print_histogram`.
439	
440	Single-step bump 0.3.4 → 0.3.5.
441	
```

1	use libc;
2	use std::hint::black_box;
3	use std::sync::mpsc;
4	use std::thread;
5	
6	use crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};
7	use crate::pin;
8	
9	pub const NAME: &str = "mpsc-2t";
10	
11	pub struct StdMpsc2Thread {
12	    req_tx: mpsc::Sender<u64>,
13	    resp_rx: mpsc::Receiver<u64>,
14	    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,
15	    counter: u64,
16	}
17	
18	fn cid(prompt: &str) {
19	    let cid = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() };
20	    println!("{}: cid={:?}", prompt, cid);
21	}
22	
23	impl StdMpsc2Thread {
24	    pub fn new(worker_cpu: Option<usize>) -> Self {
25	        println!("StdMpsc2Thread: worker_cpu={worker_cpu:?}");
26	        let (req_tx, req_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();
27	        let (resp_tx, resp_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();
28	        cid("StdMpsc2Thread before spawn");
29	        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {
30	            cid("StdMpsc2Thread before pin_current");
31	            pin::pin_current(worker_cpu);
32	            cid("StdMpsc2Thread after  pin_current");
33	            while let Ok(v) = req_rx.recv() {
34	                if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {
35	                    break;
36	                }
37	            }
38	        });
39	        Self {
40	            req_tx,
41	            resp_rx,
42	            worker: Some(worker),
43	            counter: 0,
44	        }
45	    }
46	}
47	
48	impl Bench for StdMpsc2Thread {
49	    fn name(&self) -> &str {
50	        "std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads)"
51	    }
52	
53	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {
54	        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);
55	        self.req_tx.send(self.counter).unwrap();
56	        let v = self.resp_rx.recv().unwrap();
57	        black_box(v)
58	    }
59	}
60	
61	impl Drop for StdMpsc2Thread {
62	    fn drop(&mut self) {
63	        cid("StdMpsc2Thread drop +");
64	        // Replace req_tx with a dummy so we can drop the real one;
65	        // worker's recv() then returns Err and the worker exits.
66	        // Done this way (instead of Option<Sender>) to keep step() branch-free.
67	        let (dummy_tx, _) = mpsc::channel();
68	        drop(std::mem::replace(&mut self.req_tx, dummy_tx));
69	        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {
70	            cid("StdMpsc2Thread drop wait for worker");
71	            let _ = worker.join();
72	            cid("StdMpsc2Thread drop wait for worker done");
73	        }
74	        cid("StdMpsc2Thread drop -");
75	    }
76	}
77	
78	pub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {
79	    cid("mpsc_2t.run +:");
80	    println!("mspc_2t run: cfg: {:?}", cfg);
81	    let mut bench = StdMpsc2Thread::new(cfg.core_for(1));
82	    let (hist, iterations, inner, duration_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);
83	    harness::print_histogram(
84	        bench.name(),
85	        iterations,
86	        inner,
87	        duration_s,
88	        &hist,
89	        cfg.overhead,
90	    );
91	    cid("mpsc_2t.run -:");
92	}
93	
```

1	use std::hint::black_box;
2	
3	use crate::harness::Bench;
4	
5	const CAL_WARMUP: u64 = 1000;
6	const CAL_SAMPLES: u64 = 10_000;
7	const N_LOW: u64 = 100;
8	const N_HIGH: u64 = 1000;
9	
10	#[derive(Debug)]
11	pub struct Overhead {
12	    pub framing_per_sample_ns: f64,
13	    pub loop_per_iter_ns: f64,
14	}
15	
16	impl Overhead {
17	    pub fn per_call_ns(&self, inner: u64) -> f64 {
18	        self.framing_per_sample_ns / inner as f64 + self.loop_per_iter_ns
19	    }
20	}
21	
22	struct EmptyBench;
23	
24	impl Bench for EmptyBench {
25	    fn name(&self) -> &str {
26	        "empty"
27	    }
28	
29	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {
30	        black_box(1)
31	    }
32	}
33	
34	pub fn calibrate() -> Overhead {
35	    let mut bench = EmptyBench;
36	    for _ in 0..CAL_WARMUP {
37	        black_box(bench.step());
38	    }
39	
40	    let min_low = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_LOW);
41	    let min_high = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_HIGH);
42	
43	    // Two-point fit: per_sample = framing + inner * loop_per_iter.
44	    // Slope between (N_LOW, min_low) and (N_HIGH, min_high) gives loop_per_iter,
45	    // intercept gives framing. Cancels TSC pipelining of the framing pair.
46	    let loop_per_iter_ns = if min_high > min_low {
47	        (min_high - min_low) as f64 / (N_HIGH - N_LOW) as f64
48	    } else {
49	        0.0
50	    };
51	    let framing_per_sample_ns = (min_low as f64 - N_LOW as f64 * loop_per_iter_ns).max(0.0);
52	
53	    Overhead {
54	        framing_per_sample_ns,
55	        loop_per_iter_ns,
56	    }
57	}
58	
59	fn measure(bench: &mut EmptyBench, samples: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {
60	    let mut min_ns = u64::MAX;
61	    for _ in 0..samples {
62	        let start = minstant::Instant::now();
63	        for _ in 0..inner {
64	            black_box(bench.step());
65	        }
66	        let e = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
67	        if e < min_ns {
68	            min_ns = e;
69	        }
70	    }
71	    min_ns
72	}
73	
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/notes/chores-01.md",
  "old_string": "Single-step bump 0.3.4 → 0.3.5.",
  "new_string": "Single-step bump 0.3.4 → 0.3.5.\n\n## Fix `core_affinity` pinning bug (0.3.6)\n\n`core_affinity::get_core_ids()` returns the calling thread's current\naffinity mask, not all system cores. After `main()` pins itself to\nCPU N (pool[0]), subsequent calls to `get_core_ids()` from worker\nthreads only see `[CoreId { id: N }]` — so `pin_current` silently\nfailed to pin any thread to a different CPU.\n\n### Root cause\n\n`pin_current` called `get_core_ids()` to find the `CoreId` matching\nthe target, then passed it to `set_for_current`. But `get_core_ids()`\nreflects the inherited affinity mask (which the main thread narrowed\nto one core before spawning workers). The target core was absent\nfrom the list, so `find()` returned `None` and no pinning happened.\n\n### Fix\n\nConstruct `CoreId { id: target }` directly instead of looking it up\nvia `get_core_ids()`. The struct is just a `usize` wrapper — no OS\nquery needed.\n\n### Verification\n\nBefore fix: `--pin 6,10` → worker printed `ids=[CoreId { id: 6 }]`,\n`after pin_current: cid=6` (still on main's core).\n\nAfter fix: `--pin 5,10` → worker printed\n`set_for_current(CoreId { id: 10 })`, `after pin_current: cid=10`\n(correct core). System Monitor confirmed both target cores active.\n\nObservation: ~60% utilization per core is expected — mpsc ping-pong\nmeans each thread alternates between sending and blocking on recv.\n`user=4.6s` vs `sys=15.6s` over 19.6s confirms most time is in\nkernel futex wake/wait, not userspace.\n\nMulti-step: 0.3.6-dbg-core-affinity retains debug prints,\n0.3.6 will clean them up."
}
```

> AGENT

All clean. Here's what changed for this first commit:

**Changes summary:**

- **`src/pin.rs`**: Fixed `pin_current` — constructs `CoreId { id: target }` directly instead of querying `get_core_ids()` (which only returns the caller's narrowed affinity mask after first pin). Debug prints retained.
- **`Cargo.toml`**: Version bumped to `0.3.6-dbg-core-affinity`.
- **`notes/todo.md`**: Added Done entry for the fix with ref [10].
- **`notes/chores-01.md`**: Added chore section documenting root cause, fix, and verification.

No changes to `src/overhead.rs`, `src/harness.rs`, or `src/benches/mpsc_2t.rs` — those already had their working-copy modifications from before (debug prints etc.), which are part of this debug snapshot.

Ready for your review before I propose the commit block.

> DEVELOPER

I think we should add a test in pin.rs, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good call. The exact bug scenario is testable: pin to core A, then pin to core B, verify B actually took. That's what the old code failed at — after pinning to A, `get_core_ids()` only returned A so pinning to B silently did nothing.

Something like:

```rust
#[test]
fn pin_current_can_switch_cores() {
    let ids = core_affinity::get_core_ids().unwrap();
    if ids.len() < 2 {
        return; // single-core machine, can't test switching
    }
    let a = ids[0].id;
    let b = ids[1].id;

    pin_current(Some(a));
    pin_current(Some(b));

    let after = core_affinity::get_core_ids().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(after.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(after[0].id, b);
}
```

This would have caught the bug — the old `get_core_ids()`-based implementation would leave us on core A after the second call, since B wouldn't be in the mask. Want me to add it?

> DEVELOPER

Yes but get_core_ids lies :)

> AGENT

Ha, right — `get_core_ids()` reflects the affinity mask, which is exactly what was broken. We need `sched_getcpu()` (which you're already using in the debug `cid()` calls) to check where we *actually* landed.

> DEVELOPER

testing ids.len() < 2 is wrong

Right — `get_core_ids()` has the same problem. If this test ever runs after something else pins the thread, the list could be short even on a multi-core machine. Use `libc::sysconf(_SC_NPROCESSORS_ONLN)` instead.

> AGENT

All 8 pass. Now the guard uses `sysconf(_SC_NPROCESSORS_ONLN)` — asks the kernel directly how many CPUs are online, independent of any affinity mask.

> DEVELOPER

how can we test the test IIRC there is a linux tool that can reduce the number of cpus an app can see?

> AGENT

`taskset` — e.g. `taskset -c 0 cargo test` restricts the process to CPU 0 only.

But there's a catch: `sysconf(_SC_NPROCESSORS_ONLN)` reports system-wide online CPUs regardless of affinity, so it'd still return 24 on your 3900X and the test wouldn't skip.

If the goal is to make `taskset -c 0` trigger the skip, we should use `sched_getaffinity` to count *available* CPUs instead of `sysconf`:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_019SZuEVpxXWXZjdvmHaswcY
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/pin.rs",
  "old_string": "        let ncpus = unsafe { libc::sysconf(libc::_SC_NPROCESSORS_ONLN) } as usize;\n        if ncpus < 2 {\n            return; // single-core machine, can't test switching\n        }",
  "new_string": "        let mut set = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed::<libc::cpu_set_t>() };\n        let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_getaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &mut set) };\n        if ret != 0 {\n            return; // can't query affinity\n        }\n        let available = unsafe { libc::CPU_COUNT(&set) } as usize;\n        if available < 2 {\n            return; // single-core or restricted by taskset\n        }"
}
```

On `derive(Debug)` for `RunCfg` and `Overhead` — I'd keep them. They're zero-cost when unused, and they're the kind of thing you want available the next time you need to debug something. The `println!("cfg: {:?}", cfg)` line that *uses* it is the debug noise to remove, not the derive itself.

```
1	use libc;
2	use std::hint::black_box;
3	use std::sync::mpsc;
4	use std::thread;
5	
6	use crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};
7	use crate::pin;
8	
9	pub const NAME: &str = "mpsc-2t";
10	
11	pub struct StdMpsc2Thread {
12	    req_tx: mpsc::Sender<u64>,
13	    resp_rx: mpsc::Receiver<u64>,
14	    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,
15	    counter: u64,
16	}
17	
18	fn print_core_id(prompt: &str) {
19	    let cid = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() };
20	    println!("{prompt}: cid={cid}");
21	}
22	
23	impl StdMpsc2Thread {
24	    pub fn new(worker_cpu: Option<usize>) -> Self {
25	        let (req_tx, req_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();
26	        let (resp_tx, resp_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();
27	        print_core_id("StdMpsc2Thread before spawn");
28	        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {
29	            print_core_id("StdMpsc2Thread before pin_current");
30	            pin::pin_current(worker_cpu);
31	            print_core_id("StdMpsc2Thread after  pin_current");
32	            while let Ok(v) = req_rx.recv() {
33	                if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {
34	                    break;
35	                }
36	            }
37	        });
38	        Self {
39	            req_tx,
40	            resp_rx,
41	            worker: Some(worker),
42	            counter: 0,
43	        }
44	    }
45	}
46	
47	impl Bench for StdMpsc2Thread {
48	    fn name(&self) -> &str {
49	        "std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads)"
50	    }
51	
52	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {
53	        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);
54	        self.req_tx.send(self.counter).unwrap();
55	        let v = self.resp_rx.recv().unwrap();
56	        black_box(v)
57	    }
58	}
59	
60	impl Drop for StdMpsc2Thread {
61	    fn drop(&mut self) {
62	        print_core_id("StdMpsc2Thread drop +");
63	        // Replace req_tx with a dummy so we can drop the real one;
64	        // worker's recv() then returns Err and the worker exits.
65	        // Done this way (instead of Option<Sender>) to keep step() branch-free.
66	        let (dummy_tx, _) = mpsc::channel();
67	        drop(std::mem::replace(&mut self.req_tx, dummy_tx));
68	        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {
69	            let _ = worker.join();
70	        }
71	        print_core_id("StdMpsc2Thread drop -");
72	    }
73	}
74	
75	pub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {
76	    print_core_id("mpsc_2t.run +");
77	    let mut bench = StdMpsc2Thread::new(cfg.core_for(1));
78	    let (hist, iterations, inner, duration_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);
79	    harness::print_histogram(
80	        bench.name(),
81	        iterations,
82	        inner,
83	        duration_s,
84	        &hist,
85	        cfg.overhead,
86	    );
87	    print_core_id("mpsc_2t.run -");
88	}
89	
```

  "new_string": "    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        // Replace req_tx with a dummy so we can drop the real one;\n        // worker's recv() then returns Err and the worker exits.\n        // Done this way (instead of Option<Sender>) to keep step() branch-free.\n        let (dummy_tx, _) = mpsc::channel();\n        drop(std::mem::replace(&mut self.req_tx, dummy_tx));\n        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {\n            let _ = worker.join();\n        }\n    }"
}

> DEVELOPER

Actually, I thought pub fn didn't need to be invoked maybe we need to make pin.rs "pub" in the module?

> AGENT

You're right — `pin` is declared as `mod pin` (private) in `main.rs`, so even though `print_core_id` is `pub` within it, nothing outside the crate can see it. But the warning is about *internal* dead code — the compiler sees no call path to it from non-test code regardless of module visibility.

Making `mod pin` pub wouldn't suppress the warning either — it's a binary crate, not a library, so there are no external consumers. The `#[allow(dead_code)]` is the right fix here.

id: toolu_01EbXf81UxWJ7qchr6o2EbHK
```
1	# iiac-perf
2	
3	Measure performance of various Inter-Intra Application Communication
4	(IIAC) techniques.
5	
6	## Design (0.2.0)
7	
8	Design a Rust app that can measure the cost of various (IIAC) techniques.
9	By IIAC I mean all communication techniques between and within
10	applications such as regular function calls vs async function calls vs
11	channels vs serde/deser of json or zero-copy messaging using io_uring and
12	potentially any other technique. It would include between threads or
13	processes or apps on the same computer or between apps on the same LAN or
14	between apps over a WAN.
15	
16	Ideally I'd like to be able to see a histogram of the range of times of the
17	send and receive costs, not just the mean/average of 10^3 or 10^6 invocations.
18	So the cost of the work should or could be something near-zero such as just
19	echoing the input, but maybe there is value in doing some constant but
20	variable amount of work to verify it's in-consequential. One thing I think we
21	need to be aware of is that if we make the work too simple the compiler could
22	just keep things in registers which would not be representative of "real" work.
23	
24	We should start simple, like comparing normal and async function calls and
25	later expand to other techniques.
26	
27	## Usage
28	
29	```
30	iiac-perf [BENCH...] [-d SECONDS] [-i ITERATIONS] [-I INNER]
31	```
32	
33	`BENCH` is one or more registered bench names, or `all` for every
34	registered bench. **With no arguments, `iiac-perf` prints the
35	available list and exits — that's the source of truth for which
36	benches the current build registers.**
37	
38	Flags (also visible via `-h` / `--help`):
39	- `-d`, `--duration SECONDS` — target wall-clock seconds per bench
40	  (default `5.0`); iterations and INNER auto-size to hit this target.
41	  See chores `0.3.1-dev1` for the empirical study behind the default —
42	  longer (`-d 30`+) gives publication-grade stability. Mutually
43	  exclusive with `-D`.
44	- `-D`, `--total-duration SECONDS` — target total wall-clock seconds
45	  across all requested benches; budget is split equally per bench
46	  (e.g. `-D 30` with 6 benches → 5 s each). Mutually exclusive with
47	  `-d`.
48	- `-i`, `--iterations N` — override total iterations (INNER still
49	  adapts).
50	- `-I`, `--inner N` — override INNER (the inner-loop count per
51	  histogram sample). `INNER=1` measures single-call latency (each
52	  sample = one step). Higher INNER measures back-to-back / burst
53	  rate (each sample = N steps averaged, hides per-call jitter and
54	  parking costs).
55	- `--pin CORES` — pin bench threads to logical CPUs. `CORES` is a
56	  comma-separated list with optional ranges: `0,1`, `0-5`, `0,3-5,7`.
57	  Treated as a **core pool** indexed positionally with wrap-around, so
58	  thread `i` gets `pool[i % pool.len()]`. Examples:
59	  - `--pin 0,1` pins a 2-thread bench to logical CPUs 0 and 1.
60	  - `--pin 0,0` co-locates two threads on the same CPU
61	    (oversubscription — measures contention).
62	  - `--pin 0-11` defines a 12-CPU pool for larger fanout benches;
63	    threads wrap over it.
64	
65	  On AMD Zen 2 (e.g. Ryzen 9 3900X, 12 physical × 2 SMT = 24 logical
66	  CPUs), logical IDs `N` and `N+12` are SMT siblings of the same
67	  physical core. `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max resource contention);
68	  `--pin 0,1` uses independent physical cores in the same CCX (best
69	  latency for channel benches — shared L3, no SMT contention). Check
70	  your topology with
71	  `cat /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list`.
72	
73	  Typical measured effect on `mpsc-2t` at `-d 10` (3900X, idle desktop):
74	  unpinned mean ≈ 7,044 ns / stdev ≈ 6,545 ns / p99.99 ≈ 74 µs;
75	  `--pin 0,1` → mean ≈ 5,636 ns / stdev ≈ 1,321 ns / p99.99 ≈ 17 µs.
76	  Tail tightens ~4×, stdev ~5×, mean drops ~20 %.
77	
78	Each bench prints a per-percentile histogram in nanoseconds with
79	both the raw measurement and an adjusted column (apparatus overhead
80	subtracted). Apparatus = `framing_per_sample / INNER + loop_per_iter`,
81	calibrated once at startup via a two-point fit on an empty bench.
82	
83	Examples:
84	
85	```
86	iiac-perf                        # list available benches
87	iiac-perf all                    # every bench, default ~5s each
88	iiac-perf min-now -d 30          # one bench, 30s budget
89	iiac-perf all -D 30              # ~30s total split equally
90	iiac-perf mpsc-2t -I 1           # explicit single-call latency
91	iiac-perf mpsc-2t -I 100         # back-to-back rate
92	iiac-perf mpsc-2t --pin 0,1      # pinned, different physical cores
93	iiac-perf mpsc-2t --pin 0,12     # pinned, SMT siblings (contention)
94	```
95	
96	## Workflow
97	
98	Commits, pushes, and finalizes follow a per-step checkpoint flow
99	designed for this dual-repo (app + `.claude` bot session) setup.
100	See [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) for the full
101	spec — single source of truth so the bot can't drift from the
102	human docs.
103	
104	## Convention
105	
106	This is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using
107	a bot to help in the development of a coding project. The goal
108	is that this main repo contains the "what", while the partner
109	bot repo contains "why" and "how". The key to the convention
110	is each change is cross-referenced to the other. Thus there
111	is a coherent story of the development of the project across time.
112	
113	The beginnings of that tool is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)
114	which currently does achieve this goal, but is being used as a
115	first test bed.
116	
117	## Cloning
118	
119	Use [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1) to clone
120	the dual-repo project. It handles `git clone --recursive`,
121	`jj` init for both repos, and the Claude Code symlink:
122	
123	```
124	vc-x1 clone winksaville/iiac-perf
125	```
126	
127	## jj Tips for Git Users
128	
129	See [notes/jj-tips](notes/jj-tips.md)
130	
131	## License
132

136	- MIT license ([LICENSE-MIT](LICENSE-MIT) or http://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)
137	
138	### Contribution
139	
140	Unless you explicitly state otherwise, any contribution intentionally submitted
141	for inclusion in the work by you, as defined in the Apache-2.0 license, shall
142	be dual licensed as above, without any additional terms or conditions.
143	
144	[1]: https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch
145

> DEVELOPER

We talked before about defauting the outer loop to time, I think we should still provide --iterations but maybe call it -o,--outer and rename iters to outer in that path. Thoughts?

> AGENT

Makes sense — the header already says `outer=` in the histogram output, so having the CLI flag called `--iterations` while the display says `outer` is inconsistent. `-o/--outer` aligns the flag name with what's displayed and pairs naturally with `-I/--inner`.

One thing to consider: `--outer` overrides the auto-sized iteration count, while `--inner` overrides the auto-sized inner-loop count. With both named by their loop level (`-o`/`-I`), the symmetry is clear. 

The current `-i` short flag frees up nicely too — no conflict.

Single-step or multi-step?

> DEVELOPER

Yes use -o,outer and -i,inner, single step with the default to time or two-step it?

> AGENT

I'd do two-step:

1. **0.3.7-dev1**: Rename `-i/--iterations` → `-o/--outer`, `-I/--inner` → `-i/--inner`. Pure CLI rename, no behavior change.
2. **0.3.7**: Default outer to time-based (already does this) — actually wait, it already defaults to time-based via `pick_iterations`. The `-o` flag is just the override.

So really this is just a rename — single-step 0.3.7 should be fine. The only changes are:
- CLI flags: `-i` → `-o`, `-I` → `-i`
- Internal field: `iterations_override` → `outer_override`, `inner_override` stays
- Help text updates
- README flag docs

Thoughts?

> DEVELOPER

I suggest 0.3.7 and 0.4.0 as we're close to end of single digit 0.3.X and defaulting to time is a big functional change

> AGENT

Good plan — 0.3.7 for the pure CLI rename, 0.4.0 for the time-based outer loop change. Clean version boundary.

```
1	use std::hint::black_box;
2	
3	use hdrhistogram::Histogram;
4	
5	use crate::overhead::Overhead;
6	
7	const WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;
8	const ESTIMATE_STEPS: u64 = 1_000;
9	const ESTIMATE_SAMPLES: usize = 5;
10	const FRAMING_DOMINATION_RATIO: f64 = 10.0;
11	const MAX_INNER: u64 = 1_000;
12	const MIN_ITERATIONS: u64 = 1_000;
13	const MAX_ITERATIONS: u64 = 1_000_000_000;
14	
15	pub trait Bench {
16	    fn name(&self) -> &str;
17	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64;
18	}
19	
20	#[derive(Debug)]
21	pub struct RunCfg<'a> {
22	    pub overhead: &'a Overhead,
23	    pub target_seconds: f64,
24	    pub outer_override: Option<u64>,
25	    pub inner_override: Option<u64>,
26	    /// Core pool for thread pinning. Indexed positionally with wrap-around
27	    /// via `core_for(thread_idx)`; empty means no pinning.
28	    pub pin_cores: &'a [usize],
29	}
30	
31	impl RunCfg<'_> {
32	    /// CPU id for the bench's `thread_idx`-th thread, using wrap-around
33	    /// over the pool. Returns `None` when the pool is empty so callers
34	    /// can treat unpinned and pinned runs uniformly.
35	    pub fn core_for(&self, thread_idx: usize) -> Option<usize> {
36	        if self.pin_cores.is_empty() {
37	            None
38	        } else {
39	            Some(self.pin_cores[thread_idx % self.pin_cores.len()])
40	        }
41	    }
42	}
43	
44	pub fn run_adaptive<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, cfg: &RunCfg) -> (Histogram<u64>, u64, u64, f64) {
45	    for _ in 0..WARMUP {
46	        black_box(bench.step());
47	    }
48	
49	    let step_cost_ns = estimate_step_cost(bench);
50	    let framing_ns = cfg.overhead.framing_per_sample_ns.max(1.0);
51	    let inner = cfg
52	        .inner_override
53	        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_inner(step_cost_ns, framing_ns));
54	    let iterations = cfg
55	        .outer_override
56	        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_iterations(step_cost_ns, inner, cfg.target_seconds));
57	
58	    let (hist, duration_s) = run_bench(bench, iterations, inner);
59	    (hist, iterations, inner, duration_s)
60	}
61	
62	fn estimate_step_cost<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B) -> f64 {
63	    let mut samples: Vec<f64> = (0..ESTIMATE_SAMPLES)
64	        .map(|_| {
65	            let start = minstant::Instant::now();
66	            for _ in 0..ESTIMATE_STEPS {
67	                black_box(bench.step());
68	            }
69	            let total = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
70	            total as f64 / ESTIMATE_STEPS as f64
71	        })
72	        .collect();
73	    samples.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());
74	    samples[ESTIMATE_SAMPLES / 2]
75	}
76	
77	fn pick_inner(step_cost_ns: f64, framing_ns: f64) -> u64 {
78	    let target = (FRAMING_DOMINATION_RATIO * framing_ns / step_cost_ns).ceil() as u64;
79	    target.clamp(1, MAX_INNER)
80	}
81	
82	fn pick_iterations(step_cost_ns: f64, inner: u64, target_seconds: f64) -> u64 {
83	    let target_ns = target_seconds * 1e9;
84	    let per_sample_ns = inner as f64 * step_cost_ns;
85	    let raw = (target_ns / per_sample_ns).ceil() as u64;
86	    raw.clamp(MIN_ITERATIONS, MAX_ITERATIONS)
87	}
88	
89	fn run_bench<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, iterations: u64, inner: u64) -> (Histogram<u64>, f64) {
90	    // 1 ns to 60 s, 3 sig figs
91	    let mut hist = Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 60_000_000_000, 3).unwrap();
92	
93	    let run_start = minstant::Instant::now();
94	    for _ in 0..iterations {
95	        let start = minstant::Instant::now();
96	        for _ in 0..inner {
97	            black_box(bench.step());
98	        }
99	        let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
100	        hist.record(round_elapsed(elapsed_ns, inner)).unwrap();
101	    }
102	    let duration_s = run_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as f64 / 1e9;
103	
104	    (hist, duration_s)
105	}
106	
107	fn round_elapsed(elapsed_ns: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {
108	    (elapsed_ns + inner / 2) / inner
109	}
110	
111	pub fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {
112	    let s = n.to_string();
113	    let mut result = String::new();
114	    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {
115	        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {
116	            result.push(',');
117	        }
118	        result.push(c);
119	    }
120	    result.chars().rev().collect()
121	}
122	
123	pub fn fmt_commas_f64(n: f64, decimals: usize) -> String {
124	    let s = format!("{n:.decimals$}");
125	    let (sign, body) = match s.strip_prefix('-') {
126	        Some(rest) => ("-", rest),
127	        None => ("", s.as_str()),
128	    };
129	    let (int_part, frac_part) = match body.find('.') {
130	        Some(i) => (&body[..i], &body[i..]),
131	        None => (body, ""),
132	    };
133	    let int_num: u64 = int_part.parse().unwrap_or(0);
134	    format!("{sign}{}{frac_part}", fmt_commas(int_num))
135	}
136	
137	pub fn print_histogram(
138	    name: &str,
139	    iterations: u64,
140	    inner: u64,
141	    duration_s: f64,
142	    hist: &Histogram<u64>,
143	    overhead: &Overhead,
144	) {
145	    // Header line: bench name + logfmt-style metadata. `adj` is the
146	    // apparatus overhead subtracted from each sample downstream.
147	    let adj = overhead.per_call_ns(inner);
148	    let total = iterations * inner;
149	    println!(
150	        "{name} [duration={:.1}s outer={} inner={} calls={} adj/call={}ns]:",
151	        duration_s,
152	        fmt_commas(iterations),
153	        inner,
154	        fmt_commas(total),
155	        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 2),
156	    );
157	
158	    // Band boundaries defined by percentiles. Each consecutive pair
159	    // forms one band; we iterate the histogram to compute per-band
160	    // stats (first, last, count, mean).
161	    let boundary_pcts: &[f64] = &[
162	        0.0, 0.01, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.99, 1.0,
163	    ];
164	    let boundary_names: &[&str] = &[
165	        "min", "p1", "p10", "p20", "p30", "p40", "p50", "p60", "p70", "p80", "p90", "p99", "max",
166	    ];
167	
168	    let n_bands = boundary_pcts.len() - 1;
169	    let sample_count = hist.len();
170	
171	    // Accumulate per-band stats by walking recorded histogram buckets.
172	    // Each bucket is assigned to the band containing its midpoint rank.
173	    let mut band_first = vec![u64::MAX; n_bands];
174	    let mut band_last = vec![0u64; n_bands];
175	    let mut band_count = vec![0u64; n_bands];
176	    let mut band_sum = vec![0u128; n_bands];
177	
178	    let mut cumulative = 0u64;
179	    for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {
180	        let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
181	        let count = iv.count_at_value();
182	        let mid_rank = (cumulative as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
183	        let idx = boundary_pcts[1..]
184	            .iter()
185	            .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
186	            .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
187	        band_first[idx] = band_first[idx].min(value);
188	        band_last[idx] = band_last[idx].max(value);
189	        band_count[idx] += count;
190	        band_sum[idx] += value as u128 * count as u128;
191	        cumulative += count;
192	    }
193	
194	    // Build rendered rows: (label, first, last, count, mean, adj_mean).
195	    struct BandRow {
196	        label: String,
197	        first: String,
198	        last: String,
199	        count: String,
200	        mean: String,
201	        adj_mean: String,
202	    }
203	
204	    let mut rows: Vec<BandRow> = Vec::new();
205	    for i in 0..n_bands {
206	        if band_count[i] == 0 {
207	            continue;
208	        }
209	        let mean_val = band_sum[i] as f64 / band_count[i] as f64;
210	        let adj_mean = (mean_val - adj).max(0.0);
211	        rows.push(BandRow {
212	            label: format!("{}-{}", boundary_names[i], boundary_names[i + 1]),
213	            first: fmt_commas(band_first[i]),
214	            last: fmt_commas(band_last[i]),
215	            count: fmt_commas(band_count[i]),
216	            mean: fmt_commas_f64(mean_val, 0),
217	            adj_mean: fmt_commas_f64(adj_mean, 0),
218	        });
219	    }
220	
221	    // Column widths from rendered strings.
222	    let label_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.label.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
223	    let first_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.first.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
224	    let last_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.last.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
225	    let count_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.count.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
226	    let mean_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
227	    let adj_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.adj_mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
228	
229	    const INDENT: &str = "  ";
230	    const GAP: &str = "    ";
231	
232	    // Header row.
233	    let first_col = INDENT.len() + label_w + 1 + first_w;
234	    let last_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + last_w;
235	    let count_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + count_w;
236	    let mean_gap = GAP.len() + mean_w;
237	    let adj_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + adj_w;
238	    println!(
239	        "{:>first_col$}{:>last_gap$}{:>count_gap$}{:>mean_gap$}{:>adj_gap$}",
240	        "first", "last", "count", "mean", "adjusted",
241	    );
242	
243	    for r in &rows {
244	        println!(
245	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>first_w$} ns{GAP}{:>last_w$} ns{GAP}{:>count_w$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns{GAP}{:>adj_w$} ns",
246	            r.label, r.first, r.last, r.count, r.mean, r.adj_mean,
247	        );
248	    }
249	
250	    // Whole-histogram summary. Aligned to mean/adjusted columns.
251	    let hist_mean = hist.mean();
252	    let hist_adj = (hist_mean - adj).max(0.0);
253	    let skip = first_w + " ns".len() + GAP.len() + last_w + " ns".len() + GAP.len() + count_w;
254	    println!(
255	        "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns{GAP}{:>adj_w$} ns",
256	        "mean",
257	        "",
258	        fmt_commas_f64(hist_mean, 0),
259	        fmt_commas_f64(hist_adj, 0),
260	    );
261	    println!(
262	        "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns",
263	        "stdev",
264	        "",
265	        fmt_commas_f64(hist.stdev(), 0),
266	    );
267	    println!();
268	}
269	
```
